2. RECOGNITION 
A.   INTRODUCTION:    Student Representatives to the Board  
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Introduce Marcarius and Asja. 
Asja Kepes, International Academy - East 
Asja was born in Sarajevo, Bosnia and Hercegovina. She has been  a 
competitive dancer for 10 years and also likes to write as a hobby.   She is a 
member of the National Society of High School Scholars and is very passionate 
about community service and working toward the common good by 
globalizing our community. 
Marcarius Coakley, Niles Community High School 
Marcarius is a senior at Niles Community High School. While attending NCHS 
he has been a starter for the Athens varsity basketball team. Marcarius has 
represented NCHS as a participant for the student leadership c onference with 
Oakland University. The Troy Kiwanis Club has also recognized Marcarius for 
his positive contributions as a student and community member. He has been 
offered basketball scholarships from several colleges and universities.

2.       RECOGNITION 
B. Facilities Study Team 
This year, the Troy School District formed a Facilities Study Team to 
conduct a comprehensive review of a ll buildings in the District. This 
team was made up entirely of members of the community, who 
volunteered and gave countless hours of their time and expertise to 
this important project. Tonight, we are pleased to recognize the 
members of the Facilities Study Team  for their exemplary service. We 
thank them for their thoughtful  and skilled analysis and the 
recommendations they gave that w ill help guide us as the District 
moves forward.   
We would also like to recognize an d thank Mike Zinn and Stu Redpath 
who were the liaisons to the Faciliti es Study Team.  The District could 
not have undertaken a comprehensiv e study like this without their 
leadership and direction.    
When I read your name, please come forward and receive a small 
plaque commemorating your service to the Facilities Study Team.
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Read names and pass out plaques.

          2. C.      04.17.12 
2. RECOGNITION 
C.  Oakland County Outstanding Teacher of the Year Awards  
The Troy School District has many outstanding teachers on its staff. This evening we 
are proud to recognize the accomplishments of six individuals who were nominated 
for the Troy School District Outstanding Teacher Award Program.  
The six nominees represent exemplary educators at every level and were nominated 
by their peers, by parents and by former and current students, all of whom talked 
about the impact these teachers have on the lives of those around them. Their 
nomination lett ers described how the professionals we honor here tonight help 
students find their voices, their passion and their love for learning.  
Would you please come forward as your name is called? (Distribute 
certificates/plants.) 
Two of our district nominees ar e sent to Oakland Schools to represent Troy in the 
county-wide outstanding teacher award program. Due to the criteria set by Oakland 
Schools, Mark Cavataio , Physical Education teacher at Wass Elementary School 
was nominated at the elementary level; Susan P ickering, Media Specialist from 
Smith Middle School was nominated at the middle school level.  Mrs. Pickering was 
also selected as the District’s overall Teacher of the Year and awarded a free one -
year lease of a car, donated through the generosity of the Suburban Collection.  The 
two candidates were chosen by a TEA selection committee to represent the District.   
At this time, I would like to invite Mrs. Pickering, and Mr. Cavataio to say a few 
words. 
_____________________________________________________ ___________      
RECOMMENDATION:  Distribute certificates and plants to:  
Debbie Brorson, Fifth Grade Teacher, Schroeder Elementary School  
Mark Cavataio, Physical Education Teacher, Wass Elementary School 
Pamela Mulligan, Third Grade Teacher, Hill Elementary School 
Gretchen Vogelei, Second Grade Teacher, Martell Elementary School  
Susan Pickering, Media Specialist, Smith Middle School  
Glenn Rexer, History Teacher, Smith Middle School

                 2. D.    04.17.12 
2.       RECOGNITION 
D. Troy Foundation for Educational Excellence  
This evening we are pleased to recognize the Troy Foundation for 
Educational Excellence and the work they do to support our schools, 
our students and our staff. The Foundation awards $500 grants to help 
fund projects that support the curriculum in every school in the T roy 
School District. This year, the Foundation was able to fund two rounds 
of grants, one in the fall and one in the spring, for a total of 110 
separate grants. That kind of support is invaluable. 
The Foundation is made up of volunteers who give of their time and 
talents to raise money and support innovative projects that enrich 
student learning. Again, these projects take place in every school in 
our District and many would not be possible without the resources the 
Foundation provides. 
At this time, I would like to introduce  Mike Cianciolo, President of the 
Troy Foundation for Educational Excellence, who will briefly  recognize 
the most recent group of grant recipients and the many diverse ways 
they are enhancing the education of our Troy School District students.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Karen Rockwell will roll names of recipients/projects 
on screen as Mr. Cianciolo speaks.

                    6. A.     4.17.12 
6. A.  PERSONNEL 
REPORT: District Health Insurance Update  
 As the result of collective bargaining with our union groups and in response to 
legislative changes, the District has made changes to the health insurance plans 
provided for its employees. This report will provide an update for the Board and 
the TSD community as to the nature of these changes and potential changes for 
the future. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report will be given b y Jasen Witt,  
 Assistant Superintendent of Human Resources.

IMPACT OF CURRENT 
LEGISLATION 
2 149
2011 Public Act 54 
Signed by Governor – June 8, 2011 
Amends PERA, MCL 423.215b 
Operative Provisions 
• No Cost Increase to Public Employer After CBA Expiration 
• No Step Increases or Degree Lane Changes until the parties reach agreement 
that provides these increases/changes 
• No Insurance Cost Increases (to Public Employer) until the parties reach 
agreement that provides for employer coverage of the increases 
• No Retroactive Pay Increases 
• What Happens if Insurance Costs Increase? 
• Employees Pay for Increases 
• Employer Deducts Increases from Wages 
3 150
2011 PA 152 
Signed by Governor – September 27, 2011 
Publicly Funded Health Insurance Contribution Act, MCL 15.561, et/ seq. 
Operative Provisions 
• Applies to “Medical Benefit Plan Coverage Year” on or after January 1, 2012 
• Hard Cap Provision - Prohibits Public Employers from Paying More for Employee  Medical 
Insurance Costs than Total of $5,500 Times Number of Employees with Single Coverage, $11,000 
Times Number of Employees with Employee + Spouse and $15,000 Times Number of Employees 
with Family Coverage 
• Hard Cap Adjusts Annually, Based on Change in Medical Care Component of CPI 
4 151
Main Ideas of Public Act 152 
80/20 Option 
• Elective – Requires a Board Resolution (2/3 vote)  
• Prohibits Public Employer from Paying More than 80% of T otal Employee 
Insurance Costs 
Grandfathering 
• Does not immediately apply to CBAs in Effect on or Before September 15, 
2011 
• Extensions not Grandfathered 
Deductions from Wages 
Penalty for Non-Compliance, 10% of State Aid

Unique Traits of HSA Eligible Plans 
Designed to help members become better 
consumers of healthcare, while still allowing 
protection against catastrophic medical expenses.   
• Minimum deductible $1,200 single; $2,400 family 
•  for doctor visits or prescriptions, deductible is 
applied 
• Preventative services are covered at 100%  
• Usually offered in conjunction with an Health Savings Account 
• Has a yearly out-of-pocket maximum

Health Savings Account Facts 
Your Health Savings Account will be held 
at a bank employer designates 
Works just 
like a

checking 
account – all 
funds are

controlled by

employee!

is owned by

and stays 
with the

even after 
employment 
ends 
The balance 
rolls over 
from year to 
year .  The 
money can be 
used now or 
saved for 
future 
medical costs 
You are not 
eligible if 
covered by

insurance – 
including

the money

 7. B.   04.17.12 
7. CURRICULUM 
B.      REPORT:  District MEAP Results  
The Troy School District has a comprehensive assessment 
system that includes district and school developed common 
assessments as well as other student achievement measures.  
One part of our district’s assessment program is the Michigan 
Educational Assessment Program—also called “the MEAP.” 
The MEAP assesses students in grades 3  through 9 in math, 
reading, science, and social studies.  Results of the MEAP are 
used to monitor continuous improvement.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report will be given by Anne 
Mull, Director of Instruction and Assessmen t.

10. OTHER 
 A.   First Reading:   Revisions to Board Policies B-1209, Board 
 Member Abstentions from Voting, and K -1301, Distribution 
of Materials to Students 
 Members of the Board Policy Committee have met to review and 
update Board policy B-1209, Board Member Abstentions from Voting , 
and Board policy, K-1301, Distribution of Materials to Students .  The 
proposed changes of these policies have been reviewed by the Board of 
Education’s attorney to make sure they comply with stat e law, the 
Open Meetings Act, and the Revised School Code. 
 The first reading of the proposed changes  to B-1209, Board Member 
Abstentions from Voting,  and K-1301, Distribution of Materials to 
Students is presented at this evening’s meeting.   
B-1209 Board Member Abstentions from Voting 
K-1301 Distribution of Materials to Students 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  One Board member will read changes to B-1209 and 
one Board member will read changes to K-1301.

   MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B‐1200 
B‐1209  ABSTENTION FROM VOTING 
negatively only because of personal or if a direct or indirect 
financial conflict of interest exists, or if there is an appearance of 
a direct or indirect conflict of interest, or if it explicitly is 
prohibited by any provision of the Board’s by‐laws or 
applicable law.  The nature of the conflict must be recorded in 
the minutes.  Abstention for any other reason is not permitted.  
Adopted:  April 8, 1975 
Revised:  June 7, 1977 
  January 16, 1979 
  Date Board Votes to Revise

ADVERTISING & DISTRIBUTION OF MATERIALS IN THE SCHOOLS  
K‐1300  
K‐1301  DISTRIBUTION OF MATERIALS TO STUDENTS 
  Individuals or organizations who are not or do not represent students, 
teachers, administrators, or school‐sponsored organizations of the 
District and who wish to distribute handwritten, duplicated, or printed 
materials or lapel pins, stickers, tags, or similar items on school premises 
to students or to parents through students must have the prior approval 
of the superintendent or designee. 
  Nothing herein may be construed to prevent advertising in student 
publications which are published by student organizations. 
Adopted:  April 19, 1976 
Revised:  January 16 1979 
  April 21, 1998 
  Date Board Votes to Revise

MINUTES     B‐1400 
B‐1401  MINUTES 
The minutes of Board meetings, except closed sessions, are 
recorded and printed for the secretary by the administrative staff 
and forwarded to members with the agenda for the next meeting 
for approval.  Corrected minutes shall show both the original entry 
and the correction. 
  a.  The time, date, and place of the meeting. 
  b.  The members present and members absent. 
  c.  A statement of the topics discussed and any decisions 
made at the meeting. 
  d.  The purpose or purposes for which a closed session is held. 
  e.  All roll call votes taken at the meeting. All resolutions,  
names of those who propose and second all resolutions, 
results of all votes taken including the number of those 
voting yes and no.  In the event of either a required roll call 
vote or a non‐unanimous vote, a roll call vote will be taken 
and the names of those voting yes and no will be recorded.  
The disposition of the vote will be noted.   
2.  Proposed minutes must be available for public inspection at 
the address stated on the posted notice of the meeting within 
eight business days after a meeting.  Approved minutes must 
be available at the posted address within five business days 
after the meeting at which they are approved. 
3.  All motions are recorded.  The names of those who make 
motions, those who second motions, and those who vote ayes 
and nays are recorded. 
43.  All records of the Board are made available to the public in 
compliance with the Michigan Freedom of Information Act, 
Act No. 442, PA 1976.

VIDEO PRESENTATIONS   I-1700    
I-1701 OPTING OUT OF VIDEO PRESENTATIONS 
 The Troy School District may occasionally show video of speeches, 
presentations, and enrichment activities outside the District 
curriculum. These videos are not produced by the District. 
Parents/guardians wishing to opt out of such video presentations 
must notify their child’s building principal /classroom teacher. 
  Parents/guardians will be informed of their right to opt out of any 
video presentations as part of the District’s annual notices to 
parents/guardians. 
  Reasonable and appropriate provisions will be made for students 
whose parents/guardians wish to have them removed from 
classrooms during video presentations.  
Adopted:   
204

11. A.  CLOSED SESSION 
  CONSIDERATION:  Negotiations  
The Board must pass a resolution to go into closed session. Following the 
passage of the resolution, the Board of Education will go into closed session.  No 
additional business will take place after this time, other than to officially adjourn 
the meeting. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
Be it therefore resolved  that, as permitted in the Michigan Open Meetings Act, Public Act 
267, Section 8 (c), the Troy Board of Education shall go into closed session to discuss 
negotiations.
